## 2026-11-24 · chat · accessibility, part 4: the builder and availability

- On chat/a11y-builder from origin/main c5708a7 (#246). The sweep's today-schedule findings 13, 14, 15 and 18, wired into the builder's shift sheet, its dock and the availability day sheet with the shared parts from #243; the rest of that area (11, 12, 16, 17, 19, 20) is the sibling part. No migration, no new package, no SQL, and the actions answer as before. In `packages/ui`, for every caller: `useFocusWhenGone` leaves the focus to a sheet that closes (`inClosingSheet` in `@ui/focus-after`, which `focusLost` now reads too). What goes with a closing sheet while it holds the focus (Escape on a row's button, or on an UndoRow's "ביטול" inside the sheet) used to send the focus to the page's title in a microtask, before Radix gave it back, and the Sheet then kept the title instead of what opened it. No component changed, so nothing new on `/design`; the README says the rule.
- F13, the shift sheet's "+" and "×": each is a `PersonButton` (IconButton with `useFocusWhenGone`), marked `data-add-person` / `data-remove-person` with the person, and each list's heading `data-sheet-heading` (SheetSection's new `mark`). After an add the focus goes to the next person's "+" in the same list, else that list's heading, else (the list left empty, its heading gone with it) the heading of the next list shown, the "במשמרת" one last; after a removal to the next person's "×" on the shift, else the "במשמרת" heading (`afterPerson` in `app/(app)/schedule/build/builder-focus.ts`). The press is said politely through the announcer: `app.build.added` "{name} נוספה/נוסף/נוסף/ה למשמרת, {assigned} מתוך {needed}" and `app.build.removed` "...הוסרה/הוסר/הוסר/ה מהמשמרת...", by the person's own form of address as the team's "הוסר/ה מהצוות" does (the staff carry `addressForm`; the builder's `team` type now says so), English "{name} was added to / removed from the shift, {assigned} of {needed}".
- F14, the dock after a write: a draft saved or a week published with no way back says so in the toast; once it does, where the focus was lost (the save button went, the first publish's dock went for sharing, and the board starts again from the new reading), it goes to the share button after a week's first publish (`data-share-week` on ShareWeekButton), else to `[data-builder-status]` (`afterWrite`), in an effect keyed on the write's stamp and the reading (`writtenOn` and `placed`, see After review (2)), so the write's own reading, when it comes after the toast, is met too, and no later reading or a screen shown again from a hidden `<Activity>` moves the focus. A publish of changes keeps its way: "ביטול" went with the focus and `focusAfterToast` puts it on the status line, as before. The local `focusOnScreen` and `focusLeftBySheet` are gone: `focusFirst` (and `focusLost`) do the same, the title as the last resort where nothing named is left.
- F15, the availability day sheet: the way back from a removed range is UndoRow with `autoFocus`, keyed by the range, its words from `t.markup` with the hours in Unicode isolates (as `app/push/words.ts` holds them), so "ביטול" is read with what it brings back. Undone, the focus goes to the range's first time input back in its place (`data-range`), else "הוספת שעות" (`data-add-range`). There is no hold here: the row stays until undone, another removal or the sheet's close, and closing from "ביטול" gives the focus back to the day (the `packages/ui` rule above). `app.availability.sheet.undo` is gone (UndoRow's own "ביטול").
- F18: `app.availability.fix` is "יש שעות שצריך לתקן. ליד כל יום כזה כתוב: יש כאן שעות לתקן." and "Some hours need fixing. Each such day says: Hours to fix here."; the English day line is "Hours to fix here", a twin of the Hebrew "יש כאן שעות לתקן", so the sentence quotes it word for word.
- Tests: unit `builder-focus.test.ts` (the order of `afterPerson`, the quoting, `afterWrite`), `tooling/schedule-words.test.ts` (each form of address in both new messages, the English, and the fix line quoting the day's words in both languages), `focus-after.test.ts` (`inClosingSheet`). e2e: new `builder-focus.spec.ts` on the twins (the sheet's focus after each "+" and "×" by the keyboard with what the announcer says, Escape from a "+" back to the card; the status line after a draft saved and sharing after a first publish; the day sheet's "ביטול" focused and described, the hours focused after undo, Escape from "ביטול" back to the day; the error line and the two days it points at), and in the real flows `schedule-build.spec.ts` (the status line after the draft saved, sharing after the first publish, the status line after the changes published, each as the builder starts again) and `availability.spec.ts` ("ביטול" focused, then the hours). Nothing new is written or sent beyond what those specs already do.
- Docs: ROADMAP known issue 105 without the sheet's add and remove, saving and publishing, and the availability hours; the accessibility row's part 4; `packages/ui/README.md` (the sheet rule).
- Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`. Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright (CI is the first run of the new e2e), real screen readers.
- Defaults applied, to ask Orel: (1) the last person of a list added: the next list's heading, and the last of a list with others before: its own heading, never back to the one before; (2) the words say each person's own form of address, and "נוסף/ה" without one; (3) only a first publish puts the focus on sharing; after publishing changes it stays on the status line, as it was; (4) the day sheet's way back is the design system's UndoRow (a filled row) in place of a plain line; (5) the English day line became "Hours to fix here".
- After review (seven findings, each confirmed by a skeptic; five were two gaps): (1) the write's focus moved the page: `focusFirst(afterWrite(firstPublish), { preventScroll: true })`, as `focusWhenGone` does, since the dock floats over a day scrolled down and the status line sits above the board; the focus still lands there wherever it was lost, a tap in Safari (VoiceOver's included) too, so no check that the dock's button held it. (2) It ran again on any later reading while the toast stood (another manager's write, the tab shown again with fresh props): now once per write, and once more only for the write's own reading when that comes after the answer (`writtenOn`, the reading the screen had while the write was on its way; `placed` notes the write and whether its reading was still to come), so nothing moves for a reading after that or a screen shown again from a hidden `<Activity>`. (3) The twin's status line after a save said "זו טיוטה שעוד לא נשמרה" where the focus landed (it stores nothing, and the line read only the props): WeekBuilder takes its own save from `state` as it already took its own publish (`lastSave`, the later of the two). (4) When the clock reaches the open shift, the sheet draws its people again (the locked branch puts ShiftPeople one place on, so React mounts it anew, every "+" and "×" with it): LockedNotice takes the focus in a layout effect, wherever it is lost (`focusLost`), before the button that went sends it on a microtask later, so the reason is heard as before; and `afterPerson` tries the person's own button first, so with nothing put back the focus stays on the same person, not the next one, whom Enter would then put on. ShiftPeople keeps its unkeyed place: kept, a "+" still drawn would keep the focus, and the notice would go unheard. Tests: unit `builder-focus.test.ts` (the own button first in each case); e2e `builder-focus.spec.ts` (after a save the status line says it was saved, not a draft, and the page isn't scrolled up from its foot; on the running twin with the page's clock moved on to 15:01, Omer's "+" keeps the focus as the evening begins, and with Roni taken off it the sheet's notice has it and Roni is back, kept). Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`. Not run here: Playwright, `pnpm build`.
- After the check (one finding, in this log): the F14 bullet named `arrived`, which the write's focus effect doesn't use; it now names `writtenOn` and `placed`, as After review (2) does. No code changed, so no test changed. Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`.
- After the second review (eight findings, each confirmed by a skeptic): (1) after publishing changes `focusAfterToast` still moved the page up to the status line, and `focusAfterCopy` to the copy's button by it: both now `preventScroll`, as `afterWrite`'s call does. (2) After a draft saved, a screen reader heard it twice: the status line as the focus reached it, then the toast's words a drawing later. StatusToast takes `quiet` (its region `aria-live="off"` for that message, still shown and still a status; on `/design` with a line that takes the focus, `design.toastQuietShow` "הודעה שהשורה כבר אומרת" and `design.toastQuietLine`, with their English), and the builder passes it for the write whose focus landed on that line (`quietAt`, set where the focus lands, in the same drawing the toast's words come in): after a draft saved, and after changes published whose answer came after "ביטול" went. Where the answer comes first the words are in before the focus moves, and the focus then speaks over them; a first publish lands on sharing and the toast speaks. (3) When the clock took the focused "×" away with nothing put back (all on the shift kept as it begins), or every "+" once it is over, the focus went past the line that says why to the "במשמרת" heading: that line is `data-running-line` (its stage) with `tabIndex={-1}`, and `afterPerson` tries it after the person buttons and before the headings, only for a stage later than the one the sheet was drawn at (`runningLineAfter`), so a press under way still goes to the next list's heading. (4) A press of the dock within the 240 ms after a sheet closed could have its focus taken by the card, the button being disabled while the write goes (which `focusLost` counts as lost): the close's refocus is one timer (`focusAfterClose`), and the form's `onSubmit` cancels it. (5) The once-per-write rule is `placeAfterWrite` in `builder-focus.ts`, unit-tested. (6) The twins pass `people` as the real loader does (names and photos only), so the sheet's "נוסף/ה" or "נוספה" comes from `team` there too. (7, 8) Assertions only: the shift's way back focused after a delete and the card after its undo (`schedule-build.spec.ts`), and the day sheet's hours in Unicode isolates. Tests: unit `builder-focus.test.ts` (the running line in `afterPerson`, `runningLineAfter`, `placeAfterWrite`'s cases), `status-toast.test.ts` (a quiet message's region); e2e `builder-focus.spec.ts` (the save held on its way past the close's refocus, the status line focused and the toast quiet, the first publish's toast not; the focus on the begun line once Roni's "×" goes as the evening begins; the isolates), `schedule-build.spec.ts` (the undo's focus; after publishing changes from the foot of the page, the page not scrolled up). Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`. Not run here: Playwright, `pnpm build`.
- After the third check (four findings, two of them the same one): (1) on `/design` the quiet toast had the plain one's words, so a plain toast pressed within its 4.5 s changed nothing in the region and was never said: the quiet one has words of its own, `design.toastQuietText` "הסידור נשמר כטיוטה." ("The schedule is saved as a draft."), the start of `design.toastQuietLine` now. (2) The e2e checked only that the toast's `aria-live` ended "off" (or "polite"), not under which its words came in: `noteStatusWords` (`e2e/announcer.ts`) notes each status region's new words with its `aria-live` then, and the save's test asserts the draft's words came in off and never polite, a first publish's polite. Not in `schedule-build.spec.ts`'s publish of changes: there the words come in polite when the answer is in before the toast's button goes (After the second review (2)), which depends on the local database's time. (3) The save held past the close's refocus proved the timer's cancel only if Enter came within 240 ms of the close: the page's clock now stops as the sheet closes (`pauseAt`), runs on past Radix's own refocus, and past `SHEET_CLOSES_MS` once the save is pressed and busy, and the dock's button keeps the focus. `SHEET_CLOSES_MS` is in `builder-focus.ts`, read by the e2e, no longer copied by hand. Each line of what was decided joins the live region once the focus has landed.
- F16, Today's "ביטול" rows (the first steps' "הסתרה", the bank card's "אני שיניתי" and "הבנתי"): UndoRow's `onWaitingChange` holds the eight seconds while "ביטול" is being reached (`held.pause`/`resume`), and once they are up with the focus on it, the focus goes to the next section's title (`headingsAfter` in `home/heading-after.ts`, read from a hidden anchor that stays where the card was), else the page's.
- F19: the desktop week says today in words, beside the date and in the day's name ("יום שלישי, 3.11, היום"), the filled circle as it was. The week strip reads Friday's and Saturday's time with its words ("כניסת שבת 16:23", "צאת שבת ..."), the time alone only shown, on the schedule tab and in the builder's phone strip (`DayInfo.shabbatLabel`).

- Docs: ROADMAP known issue 105 without giving and taking, approving swaps, the first steps' "ביטול" and today and Shabbat's time in the week.

## 2026-11-24 · chat · accessibility, part 2: sign-in

- On chat/a11y-entry from origin/main 0429802 (#243, the shared parts). The sweep's entry findings 1, 6, 8 and 9, wired into sign-in, joining and the demo door. No migration, no new package, and the actions answer as before. In `packages/ui` (after the check and the second review, below), for every caller: CodeSlots clears a refusal's mark on an answer that isn't one (a new code), takes `invalid` apart from the drain, drains as the screen draws, puts the focus back after a wrong code only where it was lost, and reads a screen's `describedBy` before its count; GroupedField and GroupedPhoneField take `quietMessage` (the message kept out of the field's description, nothing drawn differently). CodeSlots' states are shown on `/design`.
- F1, `/sign-in`'s phone step: a number refused (`invalidPhone`) stays on its field, which takes the focus the busy "שליחת קוד" lost, so it is read with its label, as invalid, and why; sent from the field itself (Enter, the phone keyboard's "Go"), where the focus already is, the same words are said through the page's announcer. Every other refusal (the check, too many, the wait, a failure) is a `role="alert"` line of its own right above "שליחת קוד", in the field message's style (`text-danger text-sm`), and the number is no longer marked invalid (`aboutTheNumber` in `app/(auth)/step-results.ts`). Joining has no number field: its invite step's alert only takes the key below.
- F6, the code step of both: a refusal is the code field's own description (its line's id is CodeSlots' `describedBy`), with no alert, and CodeField says each answer of its step once (`codeAnswerHeard` in `app/(auth)/step-results.ts`, as each answer arrives: the step's answer count from `useAnswerCount`, with `arrived`). Where the focus fell elsewhere (the busy "כניסה", joining's "הצטרפות לצוות") the field takes it back and is read with why; where the field kept it (the code sent itself after its last digit) the page's announcer says why, assertively, so the same refusal twice is heard twice. The count is taken as the screen draws (state from the drawing before, as `useCaptcha` keeps its control), not in an effect; it also keys the phone step's line and joining's invite-step alert, so the same refusal there is drawn anew and is never first written into the old line and then drawn again, which would be heard twice.
- F8: after each answer of the code step that leaves the person there, the code field has the focus: a refusal as above, and a new code asked for ("שליחת קוד חדש" was busy meanwhile) takes it back too, its words said after it through the announcer, politely (`resent`, the plain words from `t.markup`), the same words again included; the intro is no live region, so a new code isn't said twice. CodeField keeps its own ref to the input (CodeSlots' `ref`) and does it in a `useLayoutEffect`, before CodeSlots' own effect puts the focus back after a wrong code, so where the focus is still tells how why is said. Not as the step arrives (the field's `autoFocus` reads it whole, an error there included: the twin's `?step=code&error=captcha`), nor when the screen is shown again (`arrived`).
- F9, `/enter`: the pressed row is `busy` (aria-busy, its spinner kept) and "נכנסים בתור {name}" is said through the announcer at the press (`auth.enter.entering`, "Coming in as {name}"). The other rows aren't disabled, so the pressed one keeps the focus; presses meanwhile are ignored as before.
- Tests: unit `step-results` (which refusals are the number's, each case of `codeAnswerHeard`, the count) and `sign-in-form` (the number's refusal on its field with no alert; the others above the press, the field unmarked; every refusal of the code step tied to the field by `aria-describedby`, with no alert, and the step's only live line the digits'). e2e: `sign-in.spec.ts` (a malformed number: the field focused, invalid and described, no alert, and Enter from the field said by the announcer; the check's refusal on the twin and a failure on the real screen, a press its action doesn't know, above the press with the field unmarked, the same failure twice drawn anew; two new codes in a row inside the app's wait on the real screen, each said by the announcer, the focus back in the field), `code-slots.spec.ts` (a wrong code sent by itself: the field's description and the assertive announcer, twice, no alert; a short code checked with the press on both twins: the field focused and described, no alert, nothing announced; two new codes in a row on both twins, each a new polite line and the field focused; joining's wrong codes said each time), `enter.spec.ts` (the way in held: the row `aria-busy`, the announcer's words, the focus kept).
- Docs: ROADMAP known issue 105 without the entry part, and the accessibility row's part 2 (after the check).
- Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`. Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright (CI is the first run of the new e2e), real screen readers. Too many tries and a refusal for the check can't be reached on the local stack (the limits are lifted in seed.sql, Supabase's CAPTCHA is off), so the e2e reach the line through the twin's `?error=captcha` and a press the action doesn't know.
- Defaults applied, to ask Orel: the line for a code that didn't go sits right above "שליחת קוד", under the robot check, not in the number's card; a malformed number sent from the field itself is also said through the announcer; "Coming in as {name}" for the English words.
- After review (three findings, each confirmed by a skeptic; two were the same gap): a refusal on the code step was said twice where the focus came back from a busy press ("כניסה", joining's "הצטרפות לצוות", or a refused "שליחת קוד חדש"): once in the field's description as it took the focus, and again by its `role="alert"`. And a second "שליחת קוד חדש" in a row was never said: the intro's `aria-live` line had the same words both times, so nothing in it changed, and the field the focus came back to is described by its count only. Now CodeField says each answer of its step itself, once, as the phone step does (`codeAnswerHeard` in `step-results.ts`, in a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on the answer count with `arrived`, so before CodeSlots' own effect puts the focus back after a wrong code). A refusal's line is no alert, only the field's description: where the focus fell elsewhere the field takes it and is read with why; where the field kept it (the code sent itself after its last digit) the announcer says why, assertively. A new code takes the focus back and its words are said after it through the announcer, politely (`resent`, the plain words from `t.markup`), the same words again included; the intro is no live region any more, so the first new code isn't said twice. The answer the step arrives with isn't said again: the field's `autoFocus` reads it whole, an error there included (the twin's `?step=code&error=captcha`). `codeTakesFocus`, the forms' code refs and joining's effect are gone; CodeField keeps its own ref to the input. Joining's invite-step alert, with no field, stays. Since the announcer (in the root layout, outside `main`) now holds the same words as the screen for a moment, the e2e that look for a code-step refusal or the new code's words by text look inside `main`: in `sign-in.spec.ts`, `code-slots.spec.ts`, and also `business-switch.spec.ts` (joining's `elsewhere`), `employee-file.spec.ts` (joining's new code) and `session.ts` (`askForCode`'s new code), which would otherwise find two. Tests: unit `step-results` (each case of `codeAnswerHeard`) and `sign-in-form` (every refusal of the code step tied to the field with no alert, and the step's only live line the digits'); e2e `code-slots.spec.ts` (a wrong code sent by itself said by the assertive announcer, twice, no alert; a short code checked with the press on both twins: the field focused and described, no alert, nothing announced; two new codes in a row on both twins, each a new polite line and the field focused; joining's wrong codes said each time) and `sign-in.spec.ts` (two new codes in a row inside the app's wait on the real screen, each said). Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`; Playwright not run here.
- After the check (four findings): the short-code e2e picked its twin by the field's label, which is the same on sign-in and on joining ("הקוד מההודעה"), so its joining pass reached sign-in again and waited for a "הצטרפות לצוות" that isn't there; each twin now has its own way there (`go`). The e2e that say the same words twice in a row (a second wrong code, a second new code, on both twins and on the real screen) pressed again inside the announcer's moment for the same words (`ANNOUNCE_SAME_MS`, 250 ms), which says them once: they now wait it out first (`pastSameWords` in the new `e2e/announcer.ts`, with the announcer helpers `code-slots.spec.ts` and `sign-in.spec.ts` share); people are never that fast, so the app is unchanged there. After a wrong code, the field that "שליחת קוד חדש" gives the focus back to stayed marked invalid and red, with no why: CodeSlots now clears a refusal's mark when its status is back to idle, an answer that isn't a refusal (`refusalCleared`, as the screen draws, so the field is drawn unmarked before the focus comes back), which the other users (the clock's code, the bank's and the file's codes) never reach with the mark on, as they start again with new boxes; on `/design` a "קוד חדש" button shows it. And this entry's F6, F8 and Tests now say what the code does, not the first design review replaced; ROADMAP's accessibility row has part 2. Tests: unit `code-slots` (`refusalCleared`); e2e `code-slots.spec.ts` (a new code after a wrong one: the field focused, not invalid, read with its count only, nothing red) and `design.spec.ts` (the gallery's code boxes lose the mark with a new code). Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`; Playwright not run here.
- After the second review (ten findings, all confirmed): (1) an answer took the focus into its field from wherever the person had moved it during the wait (the demo door's link, the check's "בדיקה מחדש"). Where the focus is now decides (`focusAt` in `step-results.ts`): on the field; lost (`focusLost`, or on a press of the step's own form, busy while the answer came, which the browser moves to the page at its next drawing); or elsewhere, where it stays and the answer is said through the announcer. `codeAnswerHeard` is now `answerHeard`, for both steps, and CodeSlots' own focus after a wrong code is only where it was lost (`focusFirst`). (2) A refusal that came back after a press reached VoiceOver only as a description, which it says as a hint (after a pause, cut off by a swipe, or not at all with hints off): a refusal is now always said through the announcer, politely after the field's own read where the field takes back a lost focus, at once otherwise, and while the field has the focus as it is said, why is out of its description (`useQuietWhy`, back once the focus leaves; GroupedField's new `quietMessage` for the number), so NVDA and JAWS don't hear it twice; CodeSlots' `describedBy` now comes before its count. The number refused from the field itself (Enter) is said at once too (it was polite). (3) The code step's words (where the code went, the only place the number typed is said back) are said once as the step arrives, politely, after the field's `autoFocus` read (CodeField's `intro`). (4) The phone step starts `seen` at its first state, as CodeField does, so a send made before the page could answer it (which comes back with the page, read by its `autoFocus`) isn't said again; that such a send is refused as a malformed number even when it isn't is older and unchanged. (5) A code that didn't go for another reason gives "שליחת קוד" back the focus its busy moment lost (`focusFirst`, only where lost), on sign-in and on joining's invite step. (6) A code too short wasn't marked invalid: CodeSlots takes `invalid`, apart from the drain, and CodeField passes it for a wrong code and a short one (`aboutTheCode`); the drain now happens as the screen draws, so a field that takes the focus after a pressed wrong code is read empty and marked; on `/design` a "קוד קצר" button shows it. (7) Joining's link that stopped working on the way went back to the same page and title, silently: `JoinGoneView`'s title takes the focus as it arrives, where it was lost (`TitleOnArrival`). (8)-(10) Tests: unit `step-results` (`focusAt` with stand-ins, each case of `answerHeard`, `aboutTheCode`), the new `join-form.test.ts` (joining's code step drawn whole in each state: one live line, why first, invalid only for the digits; its invite step's line above the press; JoinForm takes `start` for it), `sign-in-form` (why first, invalid only for the digits), `code-slots` (why first, `invalid`), the new `field-group.test.ts` (`quietMessage`); e2e `code-slots.spec.ts` (a wrong code by itself: said at once, the description without why while focused and with it first after the focus left and came back; a short code with the press: marked, said once politely, then the same; the intro said once on both twins; the mark already gone at the focusin of a new code), `sign-in.spec.ts` (a malformed number: said politely, out of the description, back after the focus left and came back, Enter said at once; the focus moved to the demo link while the twin's action was held stays there and the refusal is said; "שליחת קוד" focused after each failure; joining's invite-step line drawn anew for the same failure, its press focused), `design.spec.ts` (the gallery's short code), `screens.spec.ts` (the gone twins' titles focused) and `team-changes.spec.ts` (a press on a deleted invite's open page lands on its title, focused). Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`; Playwright not run here.
- After the third check (four findings, all confirmed): (1) why comes back into a field's description once the focus leaves it (Tab, a tap elsewhere), not after a screen reader's swipe, which moves only its own cursor: the field keeps the focus, why stays out, and the visible line right after the field says it. The e2e helper (`leaveAndComeBack`), its comments, `useQuietWhy`'s and this entry said a swipe; they now say a return of the focus. The code is unchanged: no timer brings why back while the field keeps the focus, since NVDA says a change to the focused field's description, the second read the quiet keeps away. ## 2026-11-24 · chat · vacation, sickness and reserve duty (known issue 19)

- On chat/absences from origin/main efb9099. For an existing business, day to day (Orel, 22 November 2026); pay amounts stay with the accountant (known issue 18).
- Migration `20261127000700_absences.sql`: `public.absences` (whole days, four kinds, a note of up to 200 characters, soft removal, no overlap of live records, `restrict` keys and a delete-refusing trigger), read as hours are. Four writers with the secret key (`record_absence`, `change_absence`, `remove_absence`, `restore_absence`): `clock.edit` first, an unknown record answered as no access, one's own only for an owner, the person on the staff, a year back to a year ahead, never changing a day of a closed month (only the days a change adds or drops count, all of them when the kind changes, none for a note); logged `clock.absence_*` without the note. `public.team_away` gives builders and clock editors who is away and when, never why. `private.purge_business_records` takes them before the memberships. `database.types.ts` by hand.
- Domain: `@domain/absences` (parsing with the database's edges, day counts by the calendar, who is away, who records, refusals in words). The hours engine never counts days away as days worked or toward the 42; a week where counting them as a holiday is counted would move minutes into overtime is flagged `absenceWeek` with those minutes, a day away with hours `absenceWorked`, holiday pay checks `inAbsence`, the note `absencesNotCounted` (was `paidAbsencesMissing`); `PAYROLL_READINGS.absencesCountTowardWeek` (none) is the other reading in one change; `PAYROLL_ENGINE_VERSION` unchanged (no minute moves). Candidates get an `away` group, the copy an `away` reason, the activity log four lines.

## 2026-11-24 · chat · accessibility, part 1: the shared parts

- On chat/a11y-shared from origin/main f5bfd6a. The sweep's root causes that live in `packages/ui` and in shared patterns, so the per-screen parts only wire them in. No migration, no new package, no database function; `database.types.ts` unchanged.
- `@ui/announcer`: one polite and one assertive region, drawn first in the root layout's `<body>` (`app/layout.tsx`), each with its own `aria-live` from the first drawing, so a Sheet (Radix's `hideOthers` spares `[aria-live]`) never hides them and a line said before a navigation is heard after it. `announce(text, { politeness, lang })`; a line of its own each call, the same words within 250 ms once, every line gone after 7 s (`@ui/announcer-store`, no React). Never a code or an ID: CodeSlots and MaskedValue keep their own regions.
- `@ui/focus-after` (no React): `onScreen` (the Sheet's rule, moved; the Sheet imports it), `canTakeFocus`, `focusLost` (nothing, the body, gone, not drawn, disabled while it waits, in a closing sheet, a sheet's own frame), `firstOnScreen`, `focusFirst` (the first candidate that can take it, else `main h1` reached without moving the page, only when the focus is lost; a heading or line gets `tabindex="-1"`), `watchFocusIn`. `useFocusWhenGone(ref, candidates)` in `@ui/use-focus-after`, a microtask after the commit that removed the element.
- `@ui/undo-wait` (no React) and `useUndoWait`: the keyboard's focus waits with no limit, a mouse brought over it waits, a finger held on it waits, and a focus with no ring after a tap or no press at all (a screen reader's own) waits until a press elsewhere, the focus leaving, or a minute; after a mouse press it counts, as before, so existing e2e timing doesn't change. The pointer of each press is noted from the root layout on (the Announcer's module) and by the hook's module.
- StatusToast: `aria-live="polite"` on its root (F40); the undo described by the toast's words, and `action.fullLabel` (F30's base); the shared wait (F17, F35); leaving with the focus and no `onFocusLeave`, the focus goes back where it was before `focusAction` took it, else the page's title (F31's base).
- UndoRow moved to `packages/ui/undo-row.tsx` (`ui.undoRow.undo`, the same "ביטול"), its 18 importers updated, the old file gone. Still `role="status"`, with `aria-live="off"`; said once through the announcer unless its own focus says it or `quiet`, and not from a hidden shape; its button described by its words; `onWaitingChange`, `focusAfter`, `buttonRef`, `className`.
- F22, `/team/[person]`: "ביטול" takes the focus for a removal and for a deleted line, the held writes pause while it is being reached (`heldTimer` in place of the plain timers in `member-changes.tsx` and `bio-control.tsx`), undone the focus returns to "הסרה מהצוות" or "מחיקת המשפט", sent it goes to the sending line and then the "removed" card's title (or the page's title for the line); a refused removal gives the button the focus back. A held or written role change: the sheet closes onto its quiet undo (only when this opening changed it), the role row stays reachable (`ListRow disabled="focusable"`), and undone or its time up the focus is back on the row.
- ListRow `disabled: "focusable"` (aria-disabled, dimmed, its focus ring, a press refused) and `busy` (aria-busy). IconButton dims on aria-disabled. Stepper: `stepped`, says the new number after a press (`spoken` for words of a screen's own), aria-disabled at its ends so the focus stays (F21, F47). AppShell: a press on a hidden tab goes there, only the tab left in sight (`data-tab-shown`) opens the bar, counts are said on the shrunk bar too, and `needsAction` is an ICU plural (F2, F5, F10; `tabShape`, `compactPress`). CodeSlots: the digits typed and the caret's place in its own line (`codeSlotsSaid`, `ui.codeSlots.typed|at|atEmpty`), `describedBy` and `ref` for the entry part (F3). Captcha: asked for a tick while a press waits, its line takes the lost focus (`askingFocusOf`, F7). Business switcher: "עוברים לעסק X" on the press, "עברת לעסק X" when the screens are drawn for it, the focus on the title if it was lost, the pressed row busy (`arrivalOf`, F4). FileField: one live line always there (preparing, then `readyLabel`), the visible preparing line no status, the focus to the remove after a pick and to the pickers after a removal (F34; `send-form.tsx` passes `send.ready`). AvatarPhotoPicker: a ready line and the focus on "שמירת התמונה" (F50). MaskedValue: the value a live region, and the focus back on its button after the answer (F51). ChecklistItem: one range line that turns into the warning and describes the field (F45), "סומן"/"לא סומן" on a read-only check (F46), a busy photo's remove kept with aria-disabled, the focus to the take button when it goes (F42's part).
- Gallery: a toast with its undo (eight seconds, `focusAction`, then "השורה חזרה"), a toast from inside the open sheet, a section for the undo row (two rows held by `heldTimer`), one for the announcer, a row that waits for its undo. Words in he and en.
- Tests: unit `announcer-store`, `announcer`, `focus-after`, `undo-wait`, `status-toast`, `undo-row`, `stepper`, `app-shell`, `code-slots`, `list-row`, `business-switcher`, `file-field`, `avatar-photo-picker`, `checklist-item`, and `captcha-control`, `masked-value` extended. e2e: `design.spec.ts` (the gallery's toast with its undo by keyboard and by tap, over a sheet, its minute; the undo row; the stepper and the announcer; the person demo's three undos, and a tap on a phone), `tab-bar.spec.ts`, `code-slots.spec.ts`, one assertion in `business-switch.spec.ts`; `notifications`, `order-notifications` and `reminders` specs read the plural through `NEEDS_ACTION` in `e2e/t.ts`.

- Changed from the plan, following the code: `watchFocusIn` starts from where the focus is (React focuses an `autoFocus` button before its row's effect runs); a mouse resting where it was pressed doesn't count as over a row drawn under it until it moves (Chromium updates hover after layout); `focusLost` counts a focused button disabled while it waits; the title fallback never scrolls; `stepped` steps an empty field from nothing, as the stepper did; after a toast's press only the keyboard's focus still holds it; the role sheet closes onto its undo only when that opening changed the role; ListRow attaches its refusing click only to a waiting row, so a server-drawn submit row carries no handler; FileField's visible preparing line is aria-hidden (the live line says it); the switcher's test checks `arrivalOf` only (`useFormStatus` can't be pending on the server), ListRow's covers `busy`.
- After review: UndoRow is quiet (`aria-live="off"`) only as it appears, then its role's own status, so a change of its words (a way back that failed) is heard wherever the focus is, and a sheet opened over it hides it (Radix spares only what has an `aria-live` when the sheet opens); `useUndoWait` takes up a focus already inside as it listens (`waitForFocusInside`: dev's StrictMode undid the `autoFocus`'s wait); "הסרה מהצוות" takes the focus back only after a refused removal, not a role change's refusal; `useFocusWhenGone` and the removed card's title focus without moving the page (`focusWhenGone`); what `focus-after` gives a tabindex is marked `data-focus-landing`, which `app/globals.css` draws with no ring; `lists`, `chat` and `chat-announce` specs read the tab's plural (`countedTabName`, `needsActionCount` in `e2e/t.ts`), and the gallery's undo-row test checks the row lost its `aria-live`.
- After the second review: every UndoRow that can offer a second undo while the first is up is keyed by what it undoes (the gallery, departments, places, categories, shifts, positions, a template's items and schedules, templates, a sent list, an invite deleted, `/me`'s photo or line, the access screen's role switch and person, a person's role change), so each removal mounts a row of its own whose `autoFocus` takes the focus again, with its own wait and words; UndoRow's doc says `autoFocus` acts once per mount; the gallery's hold is per row, so a row going lets go of nothing newer. `aria-atomic="true"` on CodeSlots' line and on ChecklistItem's range line, so the digit or the range is said with its words. "הסרה מהצוות" after a refusal and the "removed" card's title take the focus only as it arrives (`arrived` in `@ui/focus-after`), not when the page is shown again from a hidden `<Activity>`. Button and IconButton with `aria-disabled` take no press (`pointer-events: none`, Button dims too; the chat's "older messages" button the same). The gallery's "החזרת השורות" is aria-disabled, not disabled, so it keeps the focus when pressed. Tests: unit `focus-after` (`arrived`), `code-slots`, `checklist-item`, `stepper` (and Button); e2e `design.spec.ts`, two removals in a row in the gallery and the reset keeping the focus. StatusToast's users needed nothing: a new undo there comes with a new `stamp`, which focuses its action again.
- After the check: IconButton with `aria-disabled` keeps its pointer, so a mouse over a stepper's dimmed end still shows its tooltip (`title`, "פחות"); `pressable` springs only what isn't aria-disabled, and the ghost and secondary variants keep their own fill under the pointer and the press. Button keeps `pointer-events: none` (it has no tooltip). Test: unit `stepper`.

## 2026-11-23 · chat · a link's page after sign-in (known issue 21)

- On chat/link-after-sign-in from origin/main b2b57cc. No migration, no new words, no new package. A signed-out visitor who opens a link to a screen (the week's share from WhatsApp, a list, a chat) goes through the door and lands on that screen, not on Today.
- The cookie, not `?next=`: the proxy's redirect to the door sets `ca_after_sign_in` (`@domain/after-sign-in`): httpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Secure over https, path `/`, 15 minutes (a code lasts 10). So `/sign-in` stays static, the door's phone link and the demo door need nothing passed on, the address shows nothing, and the e2e that read `/enter$` or `/sign-in$` stay true. Only for a page opened in the window (`isDocumentNavigation`: a GET with Sec-Fetch-Mode navigate and Sec-Fetch-Dest document, or, from a browser that sends neither as Safari before 16.4, an Accept that asks for `text/html`; never a prefetch's `Sec-Purpose`, and the router's headers and `_rsc` refused too), so the router's fetches just after signing out can't keep one again. The latest page opened wins: one that can't be kept forgets one kept before.
- What is kept (`afterSignInPath`, checked as the proxy keeps it and again as a door takes it): `isAppPath` (moved from notifications.ts to `@domain/app-path`, so the proxy doesn't load notifications.ts), printable ASCII, at most 512 characters, no `#`, no percent-encoded slash, backslash, dot or control character, read against a fixed base it must come back exactly as given, its path plain segments (no `//`, nothing percent-encoded), the first one of 11 screens (proxy.ts's PRIVATE but Today, the console and the four file routes), never a route handler. The security point: a link kept through sign-in comes back as the app's own request, so a route that trusts `Sec-Fetch-Site` (`/clock/pay/export`, which downloads and logs the month's pay file) would take another site's link for one of its own; `NEVER_AFTER_SIGN_IN` lists the three under kept screens, and `tooling/after-sign-in.test.ts` finds every `route.ts` and `page.tsx` so a new one fails until someone decides. A notification tapped after the session ended keeps `/notifications`: the inbox of the business the app opens in, which for someone in two businesses may not hold that line.
- Where it goes: taken by a right code (`signIn`, after `verifyCode`) and by the demo door (`enterAs`, once the session is open), through `takeAfterSignIn` in `app/(auth)/after-sign-in.ts` (plain server module); a wrong code, a resend, another number, or a refused or failed door leave it. Joining forgets it and lands where H5b says; signing out and locking a station forget it (`endSessionHere`). Signed in, the proxy never touches it. The console is never kept: the team lands on Today and goes on to `/admin` as before. Never logged.
- Screens: nothing new, the same at 390, 820 and 1440; no change to the design twin, so no `design.spec.ts` entry.
- Tests: unit `packages/domain/after-sign-in.test.ts` (every trick: schemes, `//`, backslashes, encoded slashes and dots, dot segments, control characters, non-ASCII slashes, `#`, the console, files, route handlers, `/cron`, `/api`, the doors, lengths; the inbox; the navigation check), `app-path.test.ts` (moved), `app/(auth)/after-sign-in.test.ts`, the sign-in, demo door and join actions' landing, `end-session` order, `tooling/proxy-door.test.ts` (set and delete, flags, Secure only over https, untouched for the router, a prefetch, an image, a POST, the doors and anyone signed in), `tooling/after-sign-in.test.ts`, `tooling/security-headers.test.ts` (three files name the cookie, its flags, no `searchParams` on the doors). e2e `e2e/link-after-sign-in.spec.ts`: a week's link through the code to that week; a download, the console and a planted `//evil.example` land on Today; the demo door lands on the link and signing out forgets one. `e2e/db.ts` `scheduleDemoSunday` returns the week's first day.
- Docs: ROADMAP known issue 21 reworded (the installed app on iPhone stays, not fixable in the web app), rows 41 and 50, and "מה לא נבדק".
- Changed from the plan: Next.js 16 takes the router's flight headers (`rsc`, `next-router-state-tree`, both prefetch headers) and `_rsc` off a request before the proxy sees it (`next/dist/server/web/adapter.js`, `FLIGHT_HEADERS` and `stripInternalSearchParams`), so checking them tells nothing there; the Sec-Fetch pair does, and with neither the request must ask for `text/html` (a fetch asks for `*/*`), else an older Safari's router fetches after signing out would keep a page again. The router's headers are still refused, should one reach the proxy. The path must be plain segments of letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` (so nothing percent-encoded in it at all, which also refuses `/clock/p%61y/export`), and the route check is case-blind; printable ASCII is checked first; `next-router-state-tree` counts as a router header too.
- After the second review: (1) a chat tab left open saved the last person's chat again after they signed out: on a 401 the chat's sync reloaded the whole page, a page opened in the window that the proxy kept as `ca_after_sign_in`, so the next person to sign in on that device landed on `/chat/<conversation>` or `/chat/with/<person>` and learned who the last one talked to. The chat provider now draws the screen again through the router (`router.refresh()`, as the orders' provider does), asked again with the failures counted rather than stopping the loop, should the session still be there; and the proxy keeps or forgets a page only when it is opened from outside the app's own pages (`isOpenedFromOutside` in `@domain/after-sign-in`): `Sec-Fetch-Site` same-origin (a reload, a link inside the app, the router falling back to a whole page) leaves the cookie as it was, while a phone's notification still keeps `/notifications` whatever it says, since the service worker's window may report same-origin. The other whole-page loads in the app's code go to public pages (signing out to the door, locking a station to `/station`) or are the root error screen's retry, which the proxy now ignores as same-origin; `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` lists each with where it goes, so a new one fails until someone decides, and holds the chat's and the orders' 401 to the router. Tests: `packages/domain/after-sign-in.test.ts` (`isOpenedFromOutside`), `tooling/proxy-door.test.ts` (a same-origin chat, conversation, week, Today and console leave the cookie as it was; the notification keeps the inbox for same-origin, none and same-site; none and same-site keep the page), `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts`. (2) A notification tapped after the session ended opens the inbox of the business the app opens in, which for someone in two businesses may not hold that line: said so in ROADMAP known issue 21, here, and in `@domain/after-sign-in`'s comments; no code change. Known issue 21 also says a page left open after signing out isn't kept for the next person.
- After the check: the second review's same-origin rule dropped the notification that matters most after a session ends. "התחברות מחדש לקבלת התראות" (`signedOutNotice`), the plain line, the inbox summary and the service worker's own line and fallback lead to the bare `/notifications`, not `/notifications/open/<id>`, and the worker opens them in the app's window already open (`client.navigate`, with that window's page as the source), so they come as same-origin: the proxy kept nothing, and sign-in landed on Today or on a page kept earlier. `isOpenedFromOutside` now counts the bare inbox (its path exactly, any case) as a notification too, and `afterSignInPathOf` keeps it bare, its query dropped, so a tab left on an earlier page of the last person's lines (`?before=`) carries nothing over; the inbox shows only the lines of whoever signs in. A page under it (`/notifications/settings`) is a page like any other. The other way, pointing the worker and the notices at `/notifications/open/...`, was left: it changes `sw.js`, and notifications already showing and workers not yet updated would still lead to the bare inbox. Tests: `packages/domain/after-sign-in.test.ts` (the bare inbox from every site, with and without its query; the settings page from the app's own), `tooling/proxy-door.test.ts` (the bare inbox, with `?before=`, from same-origin, none and cross-site keeps `/notifications` and replaces a kept `/lists`; the settings page from the app's own leaves it).
- Checked here: `pnpm check`. Not run here: Playwright, `pnpm build`.
- Defaults applied (to ask Orel), as recommended: a short cookie, the address clean; 15 minutes; the demo door lands on the link too; joining lands as today and forgets the link; the console never kept; a notification tapped after the session ended opens the inbox of the business the app opens in (for someone in two businesses maybe not the one that holds the line); no sentence on the sign-in screen.

## 2026-11-23 · chat · Turnstile before every SMS code (P9b)

- On chat/turnstile from origin/main 1bd2c65. Cloudflare Turnstile ("אני לא רובוט") before each of the four sends: sign-in (`/sign-in`, and the door's phone link), joining (`/join/[token]`), the holder's code to open the files and the worker's bank code. The demo door sends no code and has none. No migration, no new package.
- The token reaches the auth server as `signInWithOtp`'s `captchaToken` (`requestCode` in `app/(auth)/otp.ts`), the one call it checks: `verifyOtp`'s is deprecated in auth-js 2.119, and the admin calls take none. `sendSignInCode(phone, captcha)`, `sendCode(phone, { prepare, captcha })`, `sendJoinCode(phone, { makeAccount, captcha })`; the four actions read it from the browser through `captchaTokenOf` (1 to 2048 visible characters, else dropped). The app never refuses a send without one: it can't know whether Supabase's CAPTCHA is on, so the auth server decides. Its refusal (`captcha_failed`) is a new `captcha` error on each screen ("לא הצלחנו לוודא שזה לא רובוט, ולכן הקוד לא נשלח"), the same for every number, and is given back to the number only (`forgetRefusedSend` in `@db/auth-limits`, `public.forget_auth_attempt` with no caller): the caller's try stays counted, so token-less sends can't wear down the auth server's limit per address, which is the whole site's.
- The widget: `@domain/turnstile` (constants, the test keys, the state rules, which Cloudflare errors no retry fixes), `@ui/captcha-control` (one widget at a time, a token spent with each send and the next check started at once, a press waiting for a check on its way, never for one that failed or can't load), `@ui/captcha` (`useCaptcha`, `CaptchaBox`). Explicit render, `interaction-only`: nothing shows until Cloudflare asks for a tick, then its box with a line of ours, brought into view if a press waits. A press before the token waits ("בודקים שזה לא רובוט.") and goes by itself; the sign-in and join forms hold the submit (`holdSubmit`: React runs no action after `preventDefault`) and send it again with the button's intent as a field, since the busy button is disabled and a submit would leave it out; the file gate and the bank step await `take()`. Failed: a line and "בדיקה מחדש"; not loaded: a line, and the press goes without a token. Compact under 300px. Hebrew or English by the screen.
- The site key: `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, read with Zod in `@db/env` (`turnstileSiteKey`) by the four pages and handed down as a `CaptchaSetup`; unset, Cloudflare's always-passing test key (local, CI, e2e); another shape, a secret pasted by mistake too, throws naming the setting, so a bad value fails the `/sign-in` build. ## 2026-11-23 · chat · the file's watch after a sleep, and its e2e race

- On chat/file-watch-sleep from origin/main 1a03386, rebased on origin/main 6ee0663 (H6b, #227, merged meanwhile). #229's phone run failed once in `employee-file-holders.spec.ts` ("a locked file asks for a code again", line 279: the file's heading gone after the page's own 15 minutes). The file's own check as it opens doesn't hide the file, so the test saw the file and jumped the page's clock while that check could still be unanswered; Playwright's `fastForward` fires each timer due within the jump once, at its end, so the same jump ran the check's 10 seconds' wait out, which closed the file (`failed`) for good on that screen.
- The test, first commit (54aa8ad): `hearFileCheck` (new `e2e/file-watch.ts`) listens on the file's channel before the file opens and waits for the opening check's word that a file is in use, which the check tells the device's other tabs just after clearing its wait (the response on the network comes before the page has handled it, so it can't say this), polled for as long as the page's own wait (`KEEP_OPEN_WAIT_MS`). Three tests jump past 10 seconds with a file open, and each waits for it now: that one, the accountant's minute for the account (same spec) and the twin's minute (`design.spec.ts`, on the twin's channel). On the rebase, H6b's `openFile` already waited for the same word in its own way, for every test in the spec; it now waits through `hearFileCheck`, so the spec's two tests need nothing of their own and there is one way. The other jumps (`employee-file.spec.ts`'s and the own-ID twin's minute, `clock-code.spec.ts`'s 21 seconds) have no watched file. Every assertion kept.
- The code, second commit: the same race for a real person. A check sent (the file opening, a press, coming back), then the phone locked or the device asleep before its answer was heard, and back more than 10 seconds later within the server's 15 minutes: the overdue give-up ran and closed the file in that tab, asking for a new code, though a reload would show it open (against rule point 3). Now a give-up that runs, or an answer that comes, more than `SLEPT_AFTER_MS` (the wait and 5 seconds for a busy page) after its check came after a sleep and decides nothing: with the page in sight and no look since, the server is asked again (`lookAgain(false)`: read only, unless a fresh press waits for its minute's end), the file hidden meanwhile; a look since asks for itself, and a page put away asks as it comes back; nothing more from that check is heard (not its time left, which counted from a late answer would outlast the server's, not its word to the other tabs, not its close). A check unanswered for 10 seconds in a tab awake still closes the file there. Rule points 3 to 6 in `open-watch.ts`, `KEEP_OPEN_WAIT_MS` and `check` say so.
- Tests: unit (5, a new block in `open-watch.test.ts`): asleep with the page in sight, just past the bound and for 5 minutes, nothing closes and the server is asked once, only to read, the file then shown for what the step-up has left and closed as the server's 15 minutes from the press end; a phone locked meanwhile asks once, as it comes back, whichever runs first; the old answer (open, closed or failed), before its give-up, before the new answer or after it, changes nothing; a look slept through that meets another tab's word first on waking still shows the file; awake, on time or up to 5 seconds late, the give-up still closes. Without the change the four slept tests fail; deferring to any newer check instead of a newer look fails the word's; ignoring only answers that come after a give-up fails the "before its give-up" case.
- Checked here: `pnpm check` before each commit. Not run here: Playwright, `pnpm build`, the database: CI is their first run.
- Defaults applied: the check asked again after a sleep keeps a fresh press's slide (point 4: one kept for the minute's end is never dropped); a page still put away asks nothing on waking (a hidden tab's timers can run late without a sleep, which would ask again and again of a server that hangs, and coming back asks anyway), so a file put away whose check went unanswered stays hidden in the page, not closed, until the page comes back or another tab locks the files. ROADMAP unchanged: nothing stays open.
- Next: CI's Playwright and build for this branch (H6b, the next planned, merged meanwhile).

## 2026-11-23 · chat · sign-in tells no one who is registered, a check counts once a code went, and a day's cap (security sweep)

- On chat/sec-auth from origin/main 27b229f, rebased on 675d460 (bigger teams, #230). Three findings of the security sweep before a first real business: the sign-in screen said whether a number was registered ("not registered yet", and the auth server's "too soon", which only a registered number meets); anyone could fill a number's checks with no code ever sent to it, silently locking it out of sign-in, the file's step-up and the bank code, again and again; and there was no cap past the quarter of an hour (some 960 guesses a day at a number's code).
- Sign-in: `sendSignInCode` in `app/(auth)/otp.ts`, used by `signIn` only, answers a number with no account and the auth server's short wait as a code sent; the send stays counted, and on sign-in the short wait's try is no longer given back, since only a registered number gets it and giving it back would let "too many" come sooner to an unknown one. `unknownPhone` is gone from `SignInError` and both message files, so no path can say "not registered". This reverses the decision of 15 October (run 5, chunk 4) that an unknown number gets "not registered yet". `sendCode` keeps its answers for joining and the two file codes, whose numbers have an account; one with none there is `failed`, logged without the number. Both share `requestCode`.
- The code step's words are true either way: "אם המספר … נוסף לצוות של עסק, שלחנו אליו קוד בן 6 ספרות", "ביקשנו קוד חדש למספר …", and a help always shown under the row, "לא הגיע קוד?" with the manager hint (`auth.signIn.noCode`, a section labelled by its h2). `tooMany` says "מאוחר יותר" on sign-in, the file's code and the bank code, since a day's window makes "a quarter of an hour" untrue. The same in the three shapes `AuthScreen` gives. Twin: `/design/screens/sign-in?step=code` (`SignInForm`'s new `start`).
- Migration `20261127000200_sign_in_windows.sql`: `private.auth_limits` is keyed by (kind, scope, per), with two day rows: 15 codes sent and 30 checked per number. `public.note_auth_attempt` is sealed.sql's with only these changes: every window of a kind and scope is read, the longest wait wins; and a check is counted for its number only while a send for it was counted in the last ten minutes (a code's lifetime in Twilio Verify), otherwise for its caller only, answering -1. `@db/auth-limits` exports `NO_CODE_WAITING` and takes -1; `checked()` turns it into `wrongCode` without asking the auth server, for sign-in, joining, the bank code and the holder's step-up. Same signature, so `database.types.ts` is unchanged; the seed's update lifts the new rows too. Before it reaches a project the app sees no -1 and one window per scope, as before.
- Tests: unit `otp.test.ts` (both sends, both checks with no code waiting), `actions.test.ts` (signing in), new `packages/db/auth-limits.test.ts` and `app/(auth)/sign-in/sign-in-form.test.ts` (the code step drawn the same for any number in each state, the help in both languages, no `unknownPhone`, no exclamation marks); pgTAP `auth_limits.test.sql` (the day rows deleted inside it, a send before the checks, 20) and new `auth_limits_windows.test.sql` (35, with the review's below); e2e `askForCode` asks `dev.sms_codes` by id whether a code came (`lastCodeId`, `codeAfter`), since the screen no longer says "too soon", and `sign-in.spec.ts` replaces the unknown-number and too-soon tests (word for word the same code step, no code to the unknown number; a resend at once sends nothing and the first code still signs in) and adds a check with no code sent (`forgetSentCodes`, `noteSentCode`); `screens.spec.ts` lists the twin.
- Docs: ROADMAP row 49, known issue 67, new known issues 94 to 98 (81 to 85 are bigger teams', 86 to 93 held by the open hiring file, production and copy-week pull requests), `supabase/README.md` (the windows, the ten minutes, clearing a locked number), `packages/db/README.md`, and CLAUDE.md's sign-in line.
- After the review: a number with no account yet could still be filled with nobody seeing it, since sign-in counts what is sent to it and sends nothing, and with the day's windows some 15 sends and 30 checks a day would hold an invited person out of `/join` (whose code counts against the same number) for a day. Joining now clears such a number's counts before its code is counted: `sendJoinCode` in `app/(auth)/otp.ts`, `forgetNumberAttempts` in `@db/auth-limits`, and `public.forget_number_attempts` in the same migration (service role only, `database.types.ts` by hand). The database clears nothing for a number with an account, however `auth.users` spells it, and keeps the callers' counts; joining makes the account before its code goes, so a number is cleared until it has one and never after. Tests: unit `otp.test.ts` (cleared before the count, in one spelling; a failed clear still counts and sends) and `auth-limits.test.ts`; pgTAP `auth_limits_windows.test.sql` (a stranger fills a new number's day, joining clears it, the stranger's own counts stay, numbers with an account keep theirs, only the server clears). Known issue 95 and the migration's header say so. The rebase moved the five new known issues to 94 to 98, after the numbers the open pull requests hold.
- After the second review: (1) sign-in kept a send the auth server refused for its project-wide SMS cap or its limit per address (both read as its short wait), so while the cap was used up a stranger could fill a registered number's day with no code reaching it, and people used up their own day pressing "send a new code". The app now has its own wait between two codes to one number, the same for every number: `private.auth_send_wait` (one row, 65 seconds: the auth server's 60 by default and five to spare, since it marks a code sent a moment after the app counts it), read in `public.note_auth_attempt` from the number's last counted send, under the number's lock so two at once meet it too. A send inside it is counted for its caller only and answers -2 (`CODE_JUST_SENT` in `@db/auth-limits`), and the auth server isn't asked: sign-in answers it as a code sent, joining and the file's codes say "in a few seconds" (`rateLimit`). Past it, whatever the auth server still refuses (its SMS cap, its limit per address) and a failing SMS provider are given back for the number and the caller (`forgetAuthAttempt`, now in `requestCode` for every flow) and answered `failed`, on sign-in, joining, the file's step-up and the bank code alike. A number with no account still reaches the code step while the cap is used up or the provider is down: known issue 98, widened; 96 says 65 seconds; ROADMAP row 49 names the wait. The seed sets it to 2.25 seconds, the local auth server's 2 and a quarter, under the 2.5 the e2e wait out. (2) `/join` said too many and too soon on the invite step, so whoever held a live invite could tell whether the invited number already had an account (its counts kept, where a new number's are cleared). On the first send both are now the code step, word for word as a code that went, and that step's words are true either way: "קוד בן 6 ספרות מגיע בהודעת SMS למספר שמסתיים ב-… אם לא הגיע, אפשר לבקש קוד חדש בעוד דקה." A malformed number and a failure stay on the invite step. A new code asked for says each as it is, and known issue 94 now names what that leaves: after five sign-in codes to the number, the second press tells, and a person with an account sees the five codes. Tests: unit `otp.test.ts` (a second send inside the wait asks the auth server nothing and gives nothing back; past it `over_sms_send_rate_limit`, `over_request_rate_limit` and a provider failure give the try back and fail, on sign-in and in `sendCode`; a failed give-back still fails), `auth-limits.test.ts` (-2) and new `app/(auth)/join/actions.test.ts`; pgTAP `auth_limits_windows.test.sql` (48: one row; a second send within the wait answers -2, counted for its caller only, for a number with an account and one without; it doesn't lengthen the wait; a send given back starts none; the caller's limit still holds inside it; nobody else reads the row; the two 30-second gaps there became 90 under the test's 65-second wait) and `auth_limits.test.sql` (the wait at a second, under its gaps); e2e `employee-file.spec.ts`'s second join asks `dev.sms_codes` whether a code came and otherwise asks for a new one, as `askForCode` does, and `sign-in.spec.ts`'s "at once" test restarts the app's wait (`noteSentCode`) instead of the auth server's (`restartSmsWait`, removed), since past the app's wait the auth server's would now be a failure. Docs: the migration's header, `supabase/README.md` (the wait, the give-back, `/join`'s first answer, and a dashboard line to keep the auth server's wait a few seconds shorter than the app's), `packages/db/README.md`, and the comments in config.toml and e2e/session.ts. Until the migration reaches a project nothing holds a code inside the auth server's wait, which sign-in then answers as a failure for a registered number only: the Migrate workflow runs when this merges. Checked here: `pnpm check`. Not run here: the migration, pgTAP, Playwright. Defaults applied (to ask Orel): the app's own wait is 65 seconds on a hosted project and 2.25 locally; the auth server's limits on everyone and a failing provider read "משהו השתבש" on every flow, with the try given back; on `/join`'s first send too many and too soon read as a code that went, with the new words, and "יותר מדי ניסיונות" shows from the second press.
- After the third review: (1) `/join` still told the link's holder from its second answer whether the invited number had an account, with only one sign-in code: the app's wait runs from the number's last counted send, which a number with an account keeps from that sign-in code and one with none had cleared, so a new code asked for at the right moment read "in a few seconds" for one and went for the other. A new code asked for too soon on `/join` now reads as on sign-in, a code asked for (`resent`), and `auth.join.codeResent` says "ביקשנו קוד חדש למספר שמסתיים ב-… אם לא הגיע, אפשר לבקש שוב בעוד דקה.", true whether a code went or not. Known issue 94 and `supabase/README.md` now name what is left, all through "too many": five sign-in codes then a new code on `/join`, or one sign-in code and ten wrong checks then a check on `/join`, where the person with an account sees only one stray code; 96 says joining reads as sign-in. Test: `join/actions.test.ts` (too soon on a new code equals one that went). (2) The app's wait read only sends stamped up to the one being counted, but the app stamps a send before it reaches the database, so of two sent at once the one stamped later could take the number's lock first and the other passed the wait and both went. The wait now holds on both sides of a counted send (`a.at <= at_time + send_wait` in `public.note_auth_attempt`, the migration changed in place). Test: pgTAP `auth_limits_windows.test.sql` (51: a send stamped a second before one already counted answers -2 and is not counted for the number); every earlier expected value is the same under the new bound.
- Checked here: `pnpm check`. Not run here: the migration, pgTAP, `pnpm build`, Playwright (CI first).
- Defaults applied (to ask Orel), as recommended: 15 sends and 30 checks a day per number, beside 5 and 10 a quarter of an hour; no day's cap per address, since a business's Wi-Fi and the carriers share one; a check counts for a number for ten minutes after a send; "לא הגיע קוד?" always shown at the foot of the code step; "אפשר לנסות שוב מאוחר יותר" for too many; a new code asked for too soon on sign-in reads "ביקשנו קוד חדש" as for any number, and the earlier code still works; a number locked for the day is cleared by the team with SQL; no lock after many wrong codes in a row for now.

## 2026-11-23 · chat · a sheet closes right after a refusal

- On chat/sheet-close-refusal from origin/main 272ae2e. CI failed once on desktop at `e2e/clock-team.spec.ts` "a manager adds a shift someone forgot": ✕ pressed right after the overlap refusal left the sheet open. The cause was in the app: the sheet's close was held on `useTransition`'s pending, which a server action that calls `refresh()` after a refusal (the clock team's add, correct, cancel, bring back and break edits, `asManager`, `afterBreakEdit`) keeps true until the page read again is streamed and drawn. Next resolves the action's answer first (server-action-reducer.js, `resolve(actionResult)`, then the navigation), the refusal is set after the await, outside the transition, and shows at once, and the close pressed meanwhile was dropped with nothing asking again. A manager on a slow phone saw the error, pressed ✕, and nothing happened until the page came back.
- Fix: `useSheetWrite` (`packages/ui/use-sheet-write.ts`): `pending` is the transition's, for the spinner and the disabled buttons, so no second write goes out against old data; `held()` lasts from the press until the write's answer (a count, released in `finally`, so a throw lets go too), and the sheet's `onOpenChange` reads it. Used by `/clock/team/[person]` (every write from its sheet; the toast's "ביטול" keeps its own transition) and the clock's sheet without a code (`clock-panel.tsx`, whose punch refreshes after a refusal too). `Sheet`'s `onOpenChange` says so beside it.
- Checked and left: the pay sheets (`close-month.tsx`'s reopening, `wage-card.tsx`, `details-card.tsx`'s two) hold on their transitions, but their actions refresh only on success, where the code closes the sheet itself; the punch code sheet, the wording editor, the station lock, the list's three sheets, the availability sheets, the builder's shift sheet and its leave sheet (hidden while a publish is held, never refusing a close), the business switcher and every other `Sheet` pass a close through unheld.
- Tests: unit `use-sheet-write.test.ts` (held from the press to the answer while a refreshing transition is still pending; let go on a throw, which goes on; held until the last of two; the hook drawn). e2e: both places in `clock-team.spec.ts` (the add's overlap, and bringing a cancelled shift back over the right one) arm a `MutationObserver` that presses ✕ in the same turn the refusal is drawn, before the refreshed page can be, then expect the sheet gone and read what the refusal said; the second opens the shift again for the axe scan of its refusal, as before. Checked here: `pnpm check`. Not run here: `pnpm build` and Playwright: CI is their first run.
- After the check: on the clock screen nothing read the sheet's `pending` once the sheet without a code could close at its answer, so after a refusal (`notIn` after a clock-out on another device, say) the circle, "Start break", "End shift" and the clock-out undo could send a write against the old page before the one read again was drawn. `clock-panel.tsx` now gives them `busy`, both transitions (`pending || noneBusy`); the sheet still closes at the answer. Unit `clock-panel.test.ts` draws the panel in its four states with the press without a code on its way: every one of its own buttons waits, and each can be pressed with nothing on its way (failed before the fix). Left as it was before this branch: the code sheet and the camera, whose writes run in their own transitions the panel doesn't see, and which never held anything.
- Next: nothing left of it. Defaults applied: none.

## 2026-11-22 · chat · what a holder does in the file (profile H6b, #82)

- On chat/hiring-file-reviews from 8fa6fa8, the tip of H6a's chat/hiring-file-holders (#226, not merged yet), on origin/main c210342 (H4, H5a, H5b and H5c merged). - Bank reviews: the open file's one main action while an account waits, its words by the review ("ראיתי את החשבון החדש", "בדקתי את מספר החשבון", "בדקתי עם {first}, החשבון נכון", with what to do if it isn't theirs), through `acknowledgeBankAction` → `acknowledgeBank`. ## 2026-11-23 · chat · CI's e2e in six parts

- On chat/e2e-shards from origin/main 27b229f. Promised to Orel on 22 November: CI took about 50 minutes, most of it the desktop job's 38 to 45 (1083 tests on one worker). Each screen size now runs in two jobs side by side (`--shard=1/2`, `2/2`), six in all, each on its own runner with its own local Supabase, so no job shares the demo data with another. With `fullyParallel` off Playwright gives each part whole files, in order. Expected: about 25 to 30 minutes a run, on GitHub's free minutes for a public repository.
- The jobs are named `e2e (phone 1/2)` and so on, their results `playwright-results-<size>-<part>` and their records `passed-e2e-<size>-<part>-<tree>`; `.github/scripts/tested-tree.sh` now asks for all eight records (check, database and the six) before main skips a run. A pull request run from before this change holds the old names, so a merge of such a tree runs everything on main once. README's CI paragraph says so.
- Risk: a file that relied on an earlier file's leftovers in the same job now starts on a fresh database; CI's first run shows it. Checked here: nothing (YAML and the script only); CI is the test.

## 2026-11-23 · chat · security before a real business: files through the server only

- On chat/sec-files, stacked on chat/scale-fixes (#230, origin/main 2c50747); rebased onto main once #230 merges. Orel's order of 22 November on #1: getting ready for a real business. Three of the security sweep's findings: chat files, list photos and profile photos still had a read policy on `storage.objects`, so a session's own token, copied from the browser, could sign a link of any lifetime (working after offboarding and after a message's removal, served without the app's row check or /chat-files' rules); the avatars policy took a person's whole folder, so a replaced or removed photo whose removal failed stayed readable; and one person could push out every chat file of the business, those of chats they don't read among them.
- Migration `20261127000300_files_server_only.sql` (000200 left free on purpose): drops "people read the files of the chat messages they read", then `private.can_read_chat_file`; "people read the photos of the lists they see", then `private.can_read_list_photo`; "people and their colleagues read photos", and revokes `private.avatar_owner` from authenticated. No bucket has a policy now. New: `public.sees_profile_photo(photo_path)` (the reader's session: true only while the folder's profile points at the photo, for the person and their active colleagues); `public.set_avatar_path(person, new_path)` (the secret key's: the pointer swapped under the row's lock, the photo it replaced given back; P0002, 23514, 55000); `public.expired_avatars(at_time, max_rows)` (the secret key's: avatar files a day old that no profile points at, in the app's shape, oldest first). Replaced, each its newest body plus only the change: `private.list_photos_tick` (20261110001500_list_photos.sql's; also while an avatar is due), `public.expired_files` (20261111000700_documents.sql's; `live` is now ordered within each sender's half first, so past the space what one person holds beyond half of it goes first, their oldest first; under the space nothing changes, and one sender keeps today's order) and `public.reserve_chat_upload` (20261111000600_chat_pdfs.sql's; one check under the business's lock, between 'pdfs' and 'busy': the sender's own PDFs of the 10 minutes, with this one's 10 MiB, bring at most half the space, 54000 with hint 'own'). Three entries in database.types.ts by hand.
- Server: `chatFileBytes` and `chatPdfDownload` read the message under the session first, then the file with the secret key (no session download, no `Promise.all`); `listPhotoBytes` reads the item under RLS (its path, its photo still kept), then the secret key; `profilePhotoBytes` asks `sees_profile_photo`, then the secret key; `setProfilePhoto`/`removeProfilePhoto` go through `set_avatar_path` and remove what it gave back (tried twice, logged by code; an answer lost keeps the upload for the sweep); `cleanProfilePhotos` runs after `cleanListPhotos` in `/cron/list-photos`, each in its own try. `profilePhotoUrls` and `PROFILE_PHOTO_URL_SECONDS` are gone; `profilePhotoOwner` and `CHAT_SENDER_SPACE_DIVISOR` are new. `storedHead` keeps its 60-second link, the server's own.
- Words: `app.chat.pdfFailure.busyOwn` ("שלחת הרבה קבצי PDF בדקות האחרונות. אפשר לנסות שוב בעוד כמה דקות", `busyOwn` → retry, as `busy`), and `modules.chat.settings.fileSpaceMb.hint` says one person's oldest go first past half. Twin `/design/chat/kitchen?state=pdfOwn`, in design.spec's shapes and its PDF walk.
- Tests: pgTAP `storage_server_only` (44) and `chat_file_share` (6), new; `chat_files`, `chat_pdfs` (53: the burst rewritten, Yoav's sixth 'own', Noa's fifth goes, Shiran 'busy'), `list_photos`, `roles_lists`, `isolation` and `profile_photos` read through the rows the routes check, with every who-reads expectation kept, and "no row of the bucket" where they read Storage. Unit: `packages/db/profile-photos.test.ts`, `packages/db/file-reads.test.ts`, `profilePhotoOwner`, `busyOwn`; tooling `storage-policies.test.ts` (no policy standing, the helpers dropped after their policies, no signed read link but `storedHead`, no session Storage in the two readers, the tick names `expired_avatars`) and `chat-pdfs.test.ts`'s share. e2e: `e2e/storage.ts` asks Storage with the person's own token (sign, download, list) in chat-files, list-photos and profile specs; "a person's own PDFs of the last minutes wait"; a replaced photo 404 for her and her manager; the daily clean-up takes an avatar nothing points at and keeps the current one.
- Checked here: `pnpm check`. Not run here: the migration, pgTAP, `pnpm test:db`, the db lint, the generated types against the generator, `pnpm build`, Playwright and the screens at 390, 820 and 1440 (no Docker, no dev server): CI is their first run.
- Until the Migrate workflow runs: chat files and list photos are read as now (the row, then the secret key, while the old policies still stand); `profilePhotoBytes` reads under the session when `sees_profile_photo` is missing; setting and removing a photo update the profile as before when `set_avatar_path` is missing; `cleanProfilePhotos` does nothing; no 'own' refusal and no share. Reverting the app alone after the migration would 404 every chat file and photo (the old code reads under the session): revert both or neither. The first daily run on hosted removes every avatar a day old that nothing points at (demo resets', failed removals').
- Changed from the plan: the pgTAP's daily-job part also adds Vault's two secrets, as reminders.test.sql does, so it shows the job asks the app at a time only an avatar is due and asks nothing when none is; `chat_file_share` adds a business whose deleted account's files are newer than another's, which tells one sender from many, and checks nothing more is due once the over-share files went; Storage's list is asked with the folder as supabase-js sends it (no trailing slash); the burst's "10 minutes on" is at 08:12, past Noa's PDFs too.
